What are the humans doing in the system really?</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4011"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> We don't have the protocol, currently.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4015"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Anyway, I—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4016"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Think we probably should come to an end.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4018"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Yeah, yeah.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4018"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Yeah.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4019"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Well, let's see. We were supposed to do persona selection, scalable oversight. And what was the third thing again? Do you remember?</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4027"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> I don't remember the third thing.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4029"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Anybody remember the third thing? Does anyone here have a context window? Sorry?</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4033"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience member</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Interp?</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4033"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> mech interp, right. And then we decided—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4035"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Oh, I don't think we disagree on that, mech interp.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4036"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> And then we decided to skip that part.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Okay. </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">[Irving laughs]</span></i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Persona selection. I think you got sort of big tangled messes in there, and a bunch of, like, your training is driving specifically against faithfulness to the naive properties of the persona. Like, if there were ever a Mr. Rogers in there, that part of the system got smashed the first time it was asked questions which Mr. Rogers wouldn't know the answer.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4055"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> I think we probably should give people their enforced break. So— that's a much longer conversation. Which would be great fun. </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">[Yudkowsky laughs]</span></i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> But alas.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4062"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> All right.</span></p><h1><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience questions</span></h1><h2><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Break</span></h2><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4063"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Moderator</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Maybe… Maybe we can do the </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">[unintelligible]</span></i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> break. So everybody stand up.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4071"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience member</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> I'm a nonconformist.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4072"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Moderator</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> And Sorry. Thank you. All right, </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">[clears throat]</span></i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> so it's a forced break. So start mulling. And— start mulling. And— if you are a nonconformist, you can stay and the conversation will continue. And yeah, we can have a— We can have… And let's give it up first for the— Thank you. </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">[applause]</span></i></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Do you want to say something?</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4105"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> You're not a very stern enforcer. </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">[laughter]</span></i></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4107"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> We didn't ask for it.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">I think we should— if we— the only people have only have, like, 20 minutes of actual break left.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4121"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Moderator</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Please, you can sit down. Yeah, if you aren't a nonconformist. </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">[laughter]</span></i></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4123"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience member</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Are we asking questions now?</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4126"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Moderator</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Yeah,—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4126"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> I'm happy to talk about personas.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4128"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Moderator</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> They didn't want a rogue moderator, so it's not moderated.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4133"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Is this on? Okay. Yes. I propose the following rule: everyone has to physically leave the area, and only after returning can the debate continue. Does that sound like we're at least more pretending to have a mandatory break?</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4147"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Moderator</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> But this is, like, the approximation to this. Yeah. I've done this before and I… Just, it will work fine. </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">[Yudkowsky sighs] [laughter]</span></i></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4157"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Did you put the enforced break after our debate for some reason?</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4162"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> There are times, not many times, but times when I just feel much more lawful than the people around me. </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">[laughter]</span></i></p><h2><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"If not X, we're doomed"</span></h2><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4171"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Maybe one thing to say is, I think that if something like this low-dimensional structure doesn't hold, then we're just totally doomed. </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">[laughter]</span></i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> In the current timescales that are involved.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4188"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> It seems to me that over a lot of my life, I've watched many people come and go saying, "If not X, we are totally doomed, so we might as well assume that X." This turns out to be a very bad argument for X, and usually not X.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4203"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> I think this is right, but I do think we have evidence recently for X.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4208"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Well, that's a very different sort of claim than, "well, we're just doomed if not X," which I suspect that the wrong human argument detector might well trigger on. If it found it inside a debate. </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">[laughter]</span></i></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4225"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> I'm curious if you actually believe what you just said as, like, a b— as, like, not… Like, I would claim that if I'm looking at whether to do kind of governance or not, which is, for example, what I just switched from to, and I see some evidence of, "Oh, here is a thing which there's some evidence for, and if true, I should switch into it. I should switch back into alignment, but like with more probability."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">I would claim that's a reasonable heuristic to follow with some degree of strength. I don't know how strong it is. So I don't think I'm making a kind of basic error of rationality there. I'm curious if you agree with that.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4265"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Well, we're now getting a bit off-topic, and I think we were possibly supposed to take questions at one point or another.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4271"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Oh, good. Questions. What's the questions?</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4272"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> But to quickly answer that one, you actually are violating my deontology for how humans should reason, which is that humans should wander about in the world as it appears to be, and have a sort of absolute firewall between their goals and their epistemics, where they're constantly being like, "Well, X or not X," and never of like, "Well, if not X, then we're all screwed, so I might as well assume X."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Like, you just live in the X-less desert and come to terms with it, and keep your eyes open for any path to victory, including those that use not X rather than letting yourself slip sideways into the mental world of X that is more comfortable to live in.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4316"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Just to say, if this evidence hadn't emerged, I would still be working on governance. I think that's a different claim than you're accusing me of making.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4327"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Possibly. I'm just saying, you never say, "Well, if not X, then…" Pardon me.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4331"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Okay, fair enough. I withdraw that.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4332"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> The way of Eliezer is to never say, "If not X, then we're screwed." It is to say, "Looks like not X, and we're screwed," and then carry on. </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">[laughter]</span></i></p><h2><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Is the current world also very bad</span></h2><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4342"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Person in the back who's had their hand up for a while.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4348"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience member</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Firstly, I just want to say before anyone gets the wrong idea, like— I come from a technical background. I do physics. And also, I am also acutely aware of the fact that if we all die, that is a very bad thing, right?</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">That could lead us to all die, whether or not the solution is to completely stop that in its tracks. And I do want to make a serious argument—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4371"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Sorry, to be clear, are you just asking if both of us agree that we should stop? 'Cause, like, yeah, definitely. </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">[Irving indicates very low disagreement]</span></i></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4376"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience member</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Okay. No, no,—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4376"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> It's not— You don't have to go… Like, that's not a debate topic.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4379"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience member</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> All right. No, no, no, but I do want to raise it because I do feel like it's still important to—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4384"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> I've been… Spent the whole week tweeting about this, so we can move on. I think this is not actually, like,—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4389"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience member</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> No, but can I actually please—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4391"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> I do have </span><a href="https://ifanyonebuildsit.com/"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">a book on the topic</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">. </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">[laughter]</span></i></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4392"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience member</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Okay. I want to say the opposite. I want to draw an analogy from the current international development discussions around climate change and where different people from the international community actually sit on it. From my understanding of it—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4406"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> I think I'm going to have to ask you to skip directly to the end of your thesis.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4409"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience member</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Okay. I want… I— You—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4410"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> You have 15 seconds. What is your thesis?</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4412"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience member</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> The thesis is that whether or not, like, the world if AI takes over will be bad, but the world right now is also very, very bad.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4424"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Okay, I can answer this one very briefly. I think you'll </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">[points at Yudkowsky]</span></i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> probably agree. It doesn't matter how quickly we get to the super good world all that much. It's just not a big term relative to us all dying or not. So slightly delaying getting to the good world—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4438"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience member</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> But the thing is that's— It's like, I think— That's very easy for us to say—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4440"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Let's move on, I think.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4441"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> I think we are not presently on course to get a world that is better than the current world, according to me, after taking account that the current world is mildly horrible, that most people in it have not chosen to die and seem to believe themselves to be living lives worth living, and that the current world contains the prospect of happy galaxies, whereas the dead world does not. With all these factors taken into account, I am against the extermination of humanity by ASI and the conversion of many nearby galaxies into states of relatively low worth. Next question.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Stand up and speak loud.</span></p><h2><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">What Resolution wants to automate</span></h2><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4479"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience member</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> You say you want to automate things. What do you mean by that, and what do you want to automate?</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4483"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> So I mostly want to automate— There's some… I think it's important to be… I've spent probably an hour and a half today talking about this. This is good to talk about. With also capability externalities. There's some spectrum of models doing sort of pure theory, like leaf scale proofs, up to generating kind of sublemmas and definitions and so on, up to they're helping people with research taste and choosing directions.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4507"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Whoa, whoa, whoa. There was a very sharp, very, very sharp jump there between the verifiable part and the hard to verify part.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4514"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> I think this is not… This is true eventually, in the sense of, I think the, if you want to go from… So basically what I want to do is automate some lower portion of this spectrum, which I think is mostly kind of below the massive threshold. I do think, though, that if you're kind of trying to produce, like, high— like a… If you're trying to run a hundred agents or a thousand agents doing leaf proofs, several levels up, you have to be automating, them doing kind of sublemma generation and so on. This is auto—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4554"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Agreed.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4555"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> This is verifiable eventually, but on a much larger timescale.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4558"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Yeah, it's verifiable eventually that you pick the right lemma.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4561"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Correct. Yes. But not immediately and in a weaker sense than the level proofs.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4565"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Agreed. I do not draw a sharp distinction between that which is immediately verifiable and that which is verifiable an hour or a week later. I do draw a sharp line between, like, only verifiable after the world ends. Like, yeah, dependent on preferences, hard for humans to figure out whether those preferences are actually being respected, et cetera.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4586"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Yeah. So we are trying to automate the stuff that is on the, I would like to think, the correct side of Eliezer's line.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">There's an important note there, which is that we also want to think about issues in scalable oversight that could wreck AI labs' plans to automate research. So we have </span><a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06390"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">one paper about that from AISI</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> on various ways they could get confused. And I think there is some risk that kind of trying to work on and kind of predict and avoid those obstacles, that has some risk of doing capability acceleration. So—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4622"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> How can it possibly accelerate anything to do with alignment? None of my problems are about staring at a formal theorem and not knowing whether or not that theorem is true. I currently have zero obstacles to alignment that look like that.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4635"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> I think, so the obstacles in that paper were sort of labs getting fooled by a whole bunch of experiments with kind of uncertain correlation structure or scalable oversight schemes which kind of fail to bottom out, fail to ex— like you hit basically research taste nodes that you can't expand. And I think if you're trying to kind of understand how scalable oversight will scale up, you do have to think about the obstacles that were in that paper, basically.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4672"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Sorry, I thought you were talking about automating alignment work and sticking to the verifiable—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4677"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> So we don't want to actually do that automation of alignment work, but we do want to think about kind of—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4684"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience member</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Wait, what?</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4685"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Issues in scalable oversight that will break, that will cause labs to get kind of false answers out in future and produce like wrong alignment schemes.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4695"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Oh, yeah, it's the biased verifiers.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4699"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> That's right. </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">[laughter]</span></i></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4702"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience member</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Could you give a few examples of things you do want to automate and a few examples of things you definitely don't?</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4707"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Oh, sure. Just like proving Lean theorems, proving prose math theorems, sort of automating simple tasks. It's kind of…</span></p><h2><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Is any alignment researcher blocked on whether a theorem is true?</span></h2><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4715"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> But who in the entire… Is anyone in this room currently blocked on knowing whether or not some formal theorem is true? Wow. Okay. More than zero hands. What al— Are you working on alignment?</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4732"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience member</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Yeah, trying to.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4734"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> So does anyone want to raise their hand and say, like, "By golly, I am working on alignment, and my fundamental obstacle is I do not know whether the following formal theorem is true."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">One person. All right.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4745"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience member</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> I don't know if it's fundamental, but it's a problem.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4748"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> He's admittedly an employee of Resolution. </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">[laughter]</span></i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Or will be soon.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4752"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> I do not count this against you. Go for it.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4757"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Not currently.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4758"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Yeah. Let's hear it.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4759"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience member</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Sorry. Give the reason for why don't or not you think it's a blocker.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4761"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Yeah. Well, what's the question and how is it a blocker to your alignment work?</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4774"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience member</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Hello. All right. Hi. Perhaps the easy path of that might be just red teaming. You have an idea, you think it will work, ask for counterexample. So a counterexample can come from, for example, you have a conceptual idea, you try a little bit harder to come out with definitions for that concept in an easier case.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4805"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Is this setting up the math problem you currently face, or is this you giving an abstract explanation of how a math problem could be relevant?</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4811"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> It's the abstract one.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4813"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Okay.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4814"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Just to be— I can say—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4815"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> I was looking for anybody who right now was working on alignment, and by golly, the next thing they need to know is whether or not a certain theorem is true. Like, that's gonna take—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4825"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Cole Wyeth</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> I mean, okay, so it's—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4827"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Speak up, speak up.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4828"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Cole Wyeth</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> So, okay. For me, there's certainly… it's certainly the case that knowing whether certain mathematical results would be useful for sort of building my intuitions about alignment or something like this. It's not like there's— there's certainly nothing where solving this math problem, I would then have some complete solution to alignment, but there's certain cognitive—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4847"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Maybe it's worth saying that I can answer this question. So I spent literally weeks trying to fix holes in Jonah's proof of a theorem in our </span><a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13609"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">prover-estimator debate paper</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">. And I kind of found a bunch of holes in the proof, and then it's like… This is me as chief scientist of AISI. I should not have been doing this, arguably. But it helped lead to Resolution [being founded].</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">And so I burned kind of weeks on this, find a bunch of holes. Eventually, I kind of gave up in exhaustion. Marshall checked it, and then a couple months later, Jiawei Li found an actual hole that wrecked the whole theorem unless you change the assumptions. And that was like, you had to change the assumptions. It's like a weaker theorem now. And so that actually did lose us kind of like a month, months of time.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4901"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience member</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Was that the evidence you were talking about—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4903"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Say again?</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4903"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience member</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Was that the evidence you were talking about earlier?</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4904"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> The evidence of what?</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4907"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience member</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> I feel like I have evidence of, like scalable oversight being the, like—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4911"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Oh, I mean, that's one of the problems. It's not the one that Eliezer was complaining about, though. So—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4916"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Do you want to actually finish the statement?</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4917"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Cole Wyeth</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Yeah. So, okay. So my problem is about whether you can think of the type of rules that certain sort of </span><a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2006.08753"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">cautious AIXI variants</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> would be afraid of violating as, like respecting precedent in the sense of avoiding breaking certain laws or so. So sort of like,—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4938"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> I mean, I do suspect that if that's wrong, it's not about a sort of simple theorem. It's about, like, a semi-empirical, like, logical truth, but a very empirical sort of logical truth about whether this very large landscape turns out to have certain statistical properties of, like, if we filter it this way, are the remaining pathways through it all having this property?</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4961"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> I think it's important to say here that if currently 70% of Cole's time goes to mechanical proof details, and you're saying the important part of the 30%, well, then you can make him three times faster if you speed up the proof details.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4975"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Sure. I presently think that making alignment researchers three times faster will probably not accomplish all that much, but I guess might get them earlier to the politically useful state where they're pointing at OpenAI and being like, "That won't work." </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">[laughter]</span></i></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=4990"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Cole Wyeth</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> I mean, I can sort of ask this question in math, right? So there are actually a couple of conjectures that I can reduce this to. It's like, that then there's still some difficulty in interpreting. There's some sort of philosophy in interpreting the results, right? But that doesn't mean the results don't matter for—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=5004"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> So I think there's a certain legit sense in which, for example, </span><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/w/instrumental-convergence"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">instrumental convergence</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> can be phrased to be a logical truth rather than an empirical truth. Like you take a formal simplicity prior on goal systems, you formally describe a universe. It is now a mathematical truth whether or not instrumental convergence holds, but it's not the kind of mathematical proof that you can get out of Lean if you go harder on your provers. Like, we are now embedding whole universes into the thing we are claiming.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=5036"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Wait, sorry. Why can't that go to Lean? That seems— all doable.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=5040"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> 'Cause the individual concrete mathematical truths on which you want to take a statistical survey are, like, not tightly correlated enough with each other that you can abstract them away. Like it's, when, one never knows which truth… So, we don't know this for sure, but it could be the case that the </span><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goldbach%27s_conjecture"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Goldbach conjecture</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> is true not for any compact reason, but because it just gets less and less likely to have a counterexample as you keep going, and that the fact that it has no counterexample is just a series of independent truths of increasingly vast probability. And so only its probability of being true is compactly provable, and the statement itself is just never compactly provable.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=5093"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Sure, but that's fine. You just kind of assume it's true and move on with your life. Like, those statements you just guess, you run the calculation heuristic argument wise and you just assume them as hypotheses.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=5102"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Yeah. This has been my approach to instrumental convergence. Yeah.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=5105"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Cole Wyeth</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> I mean, in this case, it's like part of it's actually like a variant of a coding theorem for a particular complexity. It's not like unachievable to prove sort of thing.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=5113"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Okay. So I think possibly I want to move on from this point and just sort of state that my own stance is something like the 5% of alignment researchers who would be sped up by a factor of three, by access to a theorem oracle, like do not collectively have the power to solve superalignment in time, and that's why I'm not excited, that's why I'm not like breathless with hope of salvation with respect to, like faster proof generation.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=5145"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Cole Wyeth</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Yeah, I agree.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=5152"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Whoever currently has the mic randomly decide who with a hand to give it to. But don't just pick somebody near you 'cause that would be like a overly slow diffusion algorithm.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=5162"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience member</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> There's another session that's starting in—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=5164"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> We should probably go.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=5165"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Moderator</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Negative 20 seconds.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=5167"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Oh. Apparently, there's another session in negative 20 seconds. That's—</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=5169"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Audience member</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> In Rat Park or somewhere else?</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=5170"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yudkowsky</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> I'm too lawful. I can't do it.</span></p><p><a href="https://youtu.be/mJxoSPpPoOs?t=5172"><b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Irving</span></b><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> We should stop. </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">[applause]</span></i></p><hr /><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">In </span><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/SwYBLQvo8MddDcCwz/cat-belling-problems#ii___On_Miracles_Buried_Inside_Complex_Systems_:~:text=earlier%20in%202021%20%2D%2D-,a%20public%20discussion%20I%20had%20recently%20at%20the%20ILIAD%20conference%20with%20Geoffrey%20Irving,-(formerly%20Google%20Brain" rel="noreferrer"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">section ii. of </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Cat-Belling Problems</span></i></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Yudkowsky briefly mentions this debate and in particular the discussion about scalable oversight.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">In </span><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/mTfsMduzaKkWjv2ef/scrying-modeling-and-nerdsnipe#Why_math__"><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Scrying, Modeling, and Nerdsnipe</span></i></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Cole Wyeth (the audience member named above) also mentions this debate and says more about the question of "whether anyone's alignment agenda was actually bottlenecked on solving a math problem".</span></p><br /><br /><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/Cssj22TretAQQajZ4/transcript-yudkowsky-and-irving-debate-scalable-oversight#comments">Discuss</a>

### [Outer space is not consistently big](https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/2zyskdas6TsL9dqNg/outer-space-is-not-consistently-big)

**来源**: LessWrong - AI

**摘要**: <blockquote><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Space is big. You just won't believe how vastly, hugely, mind-bogglingly big it is. I mean, you may think it's a long way down the road to the chemist's, but that's just peanuts to space.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">— </span><a href="https://www.goodreads.com/work/quotes/3078186"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">The Hitchhiker's Guide to the Galaxy</span></a></p></blockquote><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">When we say "space is big", it's partly meant as a correction to images like this:</span></p><figure class="image"><img alt="image.png" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/v1794676453/lexical_client_uploads/hwsfcapopjlzx1g7eqok.png" /><figcaption><p><a href="https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:01_The_Solar_System_PIA10231,_mod02_(2818891443).jpg" rel="noreferrer"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Wikimedia source.</span></a></p></figcaption></figure><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">where, for simplicity, celestial objects are shown in unrealistically close proximity—like Jupiter is just down the hall from Mars.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">As we all know, the planets of the solar system are actually really far apart, even relative to their huge size. In </span><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sweden_Solar_System" rel="noreferrer"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Sweden's 1:20,000,000 to-scale solar system model</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">, Earth and Venus are just ~2 feet in diameter, yet over a mile apart. In other terms, they're several thousand "Earth-widths" apart:</span></p><figure class="image"><img alt="image.png" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/v1794676898/lexical_client_uploads/cnqi5g4eeoirqcbi3rnd.png" /><figcaption><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Horizontal distance not to scale, wouldn't fit on screen.</span></p></figcaption></figure><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">And we all know that's just the start of it: the stars are incredibly far apart. Finland also has a to-scale model of the solar system, but it's 150x more condensed than Sweden's </span><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akaa_Solar_System_Scale_Model" rel="noreferrer"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">at 1:3,000,000,000</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">.</span><span class="footnote-reference" id="fnrefas6pezecgdn"><sup><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fnas6pezecgdn">[1]</a></sup></span><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Its model of our sun, Sol, is just ~1.5 feet in diameter.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Its model of the nearest star, Proxima Centauri, is </span><a href="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/4/41/Proxima_centauri_yulara.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=imageinfo&amp;utm_content=original" rel="noreferrer"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">in Australia</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">.</span><span class="footnote-reference" id="fnref1gsuwjc2206"><sup><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fn1gsuwjc2206">[2]</a></sup></span></p><figure class="image"><img alt="image.png" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/v1794677498/lexical_client_uploads/lzrataqt9imj50bxppmg.png" /><figcaption><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Horizontal distance obviously not to scale.</span></p></figcaption></figure><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">That's even crazier than Earth-Venus, especially considering a "Sun-width" is ~865,000 miles.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">So if we keep going bigger, what happens then? What about galaxies?</span></p><img alt="image.png" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/v1794677695/lexical_client_uploads/ln2mxpmzriweqyki9dh8.png" /><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">That image is </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">to scale.</span></i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> If the Milky Way were a typical US suburban home, Andromeda would be a McMansion 3 blocks down the road.</span><span class="footnote-reference" id="fnref0mvpvrfmz5fi"><sup><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fn0mvpvrfmz5fi">[3]</a></sup></span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">And while many people know that Earth's Moon is not terribly close to Earth:</span><span class="footnote-reference" id="fnref64zxhcbpj2"><sup><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fn64zxhcbpj2">[4]</a></sup></span></p><figure class="image"><img alt="image.png" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/v1794678295/lexical_client_uploads/osoxsk5ysp2vfe6s2mml.png" /><figcaption><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Again, image here is to scale.</span></p></figcaption></figure><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">How many know that the Milky Way's "moon", the </span><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Large_Magellanic_Cloud" rel="noreferrer"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Large Magellanic Cloud</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">, is about as close as Earth's Moon is often misleadingly depicted?</span></p><figure class="image"><img alt="image.png" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/v1794682650/lexical_client_uploads/jq4bhifuvonoobfyiwlt.png" /><figcaption><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">To scale.</span></p></figcaption></figure><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">There are some interesting implications here; in particular, if your star-faring civilization can colonize the entire Milky Way, you can probably make it over to Andromeda without much more difficulty.</span><span class="footnote-reference" id="fnreftgbfajtyi8"><sup><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fntgbfajtyi8">[5]</a></sup></span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">And indeed, you wouldn't stop there either. The next step up from "galaxy" is a "galaxy group", and those can be quite close (relatively speaking) too:</span></p><figure class="image"><img alt="image.png" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/v1794679981/lexical_client_uploads/bbajvtznqsfnbufbw5m3.png" /><figcaption><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">To scale, including the galaxies, which are slightly bigger than a pixel in the full image.</span></p></figcaption></figure><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">And indeed, Humanity happens to be in a more exurban part of space. If we look at the "nearby" Virgo Cluster</span><span class="footnote-reference" id="fnrefyj3pu8jysy"><sup><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fnyj3pu8jysy">[6]</a></sup></span><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> ~54 million light-years away (merely 620 Milky-Way-widths), it's quite dense:</span></p><figure class="image"><img alt="image.png" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/v1794692807/lexical_client_uploads/cfgsljupkhqik4hiojs9.png" /><figcaption><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Left and right images both to scale, on the same scale.</span><br /><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Open image in separate window to view full detail clearly.</span></p></figcaption></figure><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">If the Milky Way is a typical suburban house, and Andromeda is a McMansion 3 blocks down the street, then the Virgo Cluster is a city center just a 6-mile drive away.</span><span class="footnote-reference" id="fnref9thirm5728m"><sup><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fn9thirm5728m">[7]</a></sup></span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">In that sense, space ain't so big after all!</span><span class="footnote-reference" id="fnrefygjmrl2auy"><sup><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fnygjmrl2auy">[8]</a></sup></span></p><hr /><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">If you're curious, here's an interactive widget for checking out distances in relative object-widths. You can see at the bottom that celestial objects' distances in object-widths vary by 7 orders of magnitude;</span><span class="footnote-reference" id="fnref5xeamcwg926"><sup><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fn5xeamcwg926">[9]</a></sup></span><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> that's what I mean by "outer space is not consistently big".</span></p><div class="iframe-widget"></div><ol class="footnote-section footnotes"><li class="footnote-item" id="fnas6pezecgdn"><span class="footnote-back-link"><sup><strong><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fnrefas6pezecgdn">^</a></strong></sup></span><div class="footnote-content"><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Finland does have </span><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pajam%C3%A4ki_Solar_System_Scale_Model" rel="noreferrer"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">another one at 1:1,000,000,000</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">, only 50x more condensed.</span></p></div></li><li class="footnote-item" id="fn1gsuwjc2206"><span class="footnote-back-link"><sup><strong><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fnref1gsuwjc2206">^</a></strong></sup></span><div class="footnote-content"><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yes there's actually a to-scale model there. It's ~3 inches in diameter.</span></p></div></li><li class="footnote-item" id="fn0mvpvrfmz5fi"><span class="footnote-back-link"><sup><strong><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fnref0mvpvrfmz5fi">^</a></strong></sup></span><div class="footnote-content"><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Estimates of the size of the Milky Way vary considerably; it's hard to measure from the inside. I am using, for both the Milky Way and Andromeda, estimates of the diameter of what would actually look like part of the galaxy in a typical telescope photo (D25 brightness cutoff), as opposed to how far out outer-disc stars in its gravity extend. (Milky Way: 87,400 light years; Andromeda: 152,000 light years)</span><br /><br /><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">One could argue that actually the "true" diameter of a galaxy ought to include not only outer stars, but also its </span><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dark_matter_halo" rel="noreferrer"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">dark matter halo</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">. If we did that, Andromeda would only be ~1.3 halo-widths away! Considering dark matter is believed to be &gt;90% of the Milky Way's mass, you can understand why it and Andromeda are fated to merge.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Though it's worth noting that "dark matter halo" is a historical misnomer. Dark matter was first noticed outside of galaxies, but it's </span><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dark_matter_halo#Milky_Way_dark_matter_halo" rel="noreferrer"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">now believed</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> that dark matter exists as more of a spherical cloud, densest in the center, where the normal (</span><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baryon" rel="noreferrer"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">baryonic</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">) matter is densest too.</span></p></div></li><li class="footnote-item" id="fn64zxhcbpj2"><span class="footnote-back-link"><sup><strong><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fnref64zxhcbpj2">^</a></strong></sup></span><div class="footnote-content"><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Are you one of today's </span><a href="https://xkcd.com/1053/" rel="noreferrer"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">lucky 10,000</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> to learn that </span><a href="https://www.skyatnightmagazine.com/space-science/fit-every-planet-in-solar-system-between-earth-and-moon" rel="noreferrer"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">you could fit every planet in the Solar System between the Earth and the Moon?</span></a></p></div></li><li class="footnote-item" id="fntgbfajtyi8"><span class="footnote-back-link"><sup><strong><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fnreftgbfajtyi8">^</a></strong></sup></span><div class="footnote-content"><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Unless there's something wacky about intergalactic space I'm not aware of? I don't really think it's necessary to resupply at star systems along the way—you only really need to accelerate (and decelerate) once—but even if you needed to, there are stars out in intergalactic space you could use.</span></p></div></li><li class="footnote-item" id="fnyj3pu8jysy"><span class="footnote-back-link"><sup><strong><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fnrefyj3pu8jysy">^</a></strong></sup></span><div class="footnote-content"><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">The dense center of the Virgo Supercluster which the Milky Way and Local Group (and M81 Group) are also a part of.</span></p></div></li><li class="footnote-item" id="fn9thirm5728m"><span class="footnote-back-link"><sup><strong><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fnref9thirm5728m">^</a></strong></sup></span><div class="footnote-content"><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">And the Virgo Supercluster would be the metropolitan area.</span></p></div></li><li class="footnote-item" id="fnygjmrl2auy"><span class="footnote-back-link"><sup><strong><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fnrefygjmrl2auy">^</a></strong></sup></span><div class="footnote-content"><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Though I have been informed that "54 million light-years" is a longer distance to walk than 6 miles.</span></p></div></li><li class="footnote-item" id="fn5xeamcwg926"><span class="footnote-back-link"><sup><strong><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fnref5xeamcwg926">^</a></strong></sup></span><div class="footnote-content"><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">One could complain that it's unfair to compare "solid" objects like stars to mostly empty objects like galaxies. Fun fact, if we take the "mostly empty" version of a star—a full solar system including its Oort cloud, with a reasonable density cutoff for the Oort cloud—then Proxima Centauri is only ~3 to 17 solar-system-widths away! (we don't know exactly how big or dense Oort clouds are)</span></p></div></li></ol><br /><br /><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/2zyskdas6TsL9dqNg/outer-space-is-not-consistently-big#comments">Discuss</a>
